Summary 215
Summary
The Monitoring of China’s Advanced Technology in 2021 and Building a
Database: Focused on Smart Education and Digital Healthcare
ㆍProject Leader: Seoin Baek
ㆍParticipants: Park dongun· Son eunjeong· Kim youngjin· Yoon Yeojin
This study explicated China’s core competencies in the field of digital services,
especially in the field of smart education and digital healthcare. China’s strength in most
digital core technologies such as AI and 5G is that it preemptively applies and utilizes core
technologies in line with negative regulatory systems. In the case of smart education and
digital healthcare, which are highly utilized by AI technology, China’s technology, market,
and institutional capabilities are combined to promote innovation rapidly.
This study aims to analyze technology, markets, institutions, and innovation ecosystems
(leading core players) in China’s smart education and digital healthcare industries, derive
implications, and seek answers to the following research questions.
 How is digital service (smart education, digital healthcare) different from traditional
education and medical services or services that became online?
 Has China’s digital service (smart education, digital healthcare) sector achieved
innovation? How is it different from other fields/country’s innovation patterns and why
was it possible?
 Who is the innovator of Chinese digital services (smart education, digital healthcare), and
how are they interacting with and developing technology/market/institutional changes?
 How are the patterns of innovation in the digital service sector different between Korea
and China? What are some difficulties in cooperation in digital service innovation
between Korea and China? How should Korea-China digital service innovation
cooperation be promoted?
First, for previous studies, we investigated the theories of Technology Innovation
System, Socio-Technical Transition, and Digital Transformation. Technological innovation
system theory focuses on the spread of specific new technologies through a systematic and

===== PDF_PAGE 238 =====
216 2021년 중국(중화권) 첨단기술 모니터링 및 DB 구축 사업: 스마트 에듀·디지털 헬스케어
integrated approach (holistic), and the social-technology transformation theory analyzed
the causes and processes of how social-technology systems switch to meet social needs such
as energy, transportation, and housing at a more macroscopic and long-term dimension.
Finally, digital transformation means not only streamlining the product and production
process through digital innovation, but also causing changes in technology and industry.
Infrastructure related to ICT, AI, robots, and big data are essential elements for realizing
digital transformation and securing related core technologies has become significant.
The framework of this study is based on the above theories, but the framework of analysis
suggested in previous studies was modified and used to focus on the core external enablers
of digital transformation, the actors of transformation, and institutional changes. In other
words, by adding macro-environmental factors such as demographic changes and
COVID-19 based on the technological innovation system, technological changes,
market/demand changes, and institutional changes in the smart education and digital
healthcare sectors were first analyzed. After, the ecosystem centered on public innovation
subjects and the ecosystem centered on private innovation subjects were classified, and
each technology, business model, licensing, and public purchase status were analyzed.
1. Smart Education
Smart Education means the combination of advanced ICT and learning, can include
technologies such as AI, mobile, and location-based education. Its related digital-based
learning tools are diversifying.
1.1. Environmental Changes
(1) Technological changes: The core of smart education technology is AI, which attempts
to differentiate itself by converging various technologies. Among them, machine learning,
facial recognition/eye tracking, image identification, and algorithm-based customized
program technologies are areas where Chinese companies show strengths and are actively
used in connection with education.
(2) Demand changes: China is the world’s largest market, and demand for smart
education is expected to grow due to factors such as non-face-to-face technology caused by
COVID-19, widening regional and income gaps, and abolishment of the birth control.
(3) Institutional changes: The Chinese government actively promoted education and ICT

===== PDF_PAGE 239 =====
Summary 217
convergence and continued to put emphasis on the universalization of education. Various
educational policies in China encourage the smartization of the public sector (school) and the
development of learning contents linking ICT technologies in the public and private sectors
(education companies). In the early days of COVID-19, the educational platform of private
companies was opened free of charge, and online classes of schools and private educational
institutions were actively encouraged. However, in 2021, the Double Reduction Policy
(reduce exclusive homework and off-campus tutoring for studies) was announced, and
public education reinforcement and private education suppression were strongly promoted.
1.2. Innovation Ecosystem
(1) Public actor driven ecosystem: As part of the government’s educational
informatization policy, private companies are actively participating in the school
digitalization project (smart campus). Smart campus refers to a campus that enables
efficient education and campus management by applying technologies such as IoT, cloud
computing, and big data analysis to all facilities in class and campus. Projects are underway
in nine cities, including Beijing, to create areas where you can learn without time and space
restrictions based on the smart campus project. Companies participating in public
innovation include Alibaba Dingtalk, TENCENT, iFLYTEK, Huawei Cloud, etc.
(2) Private actor driven ecosystem: Private innovation actors are promoting innovation
faster than the public sector (schools) by combining new technologies such as AI with
learning content. Existing companies (XDF, Hujiang, Offcn, etc.), Big Tech companies
(Baidu, Sense Time, etc.), and startups (Zuoyebang, VIPKID, TAL, Yuanfudao, etc.) are
participating in public innovation in addition to private innovation such as securing their
core technologies.
In summary, among the public innovation subjects, telecommunication companies and
big tech companies mainly participate in building the rudiment school environment, while
ICT companies participate in providing educational equipment such as tablets and smart
displays, mainly focusing on building the physical environment. In contrast, private
innovation actors are focusing on developing new technologies to gain an advantage in
online learning rather than building a physical environment. Startups continue to innovate
by actively utilizing technologies such as AI, VR/AR, Cloud, and Big Data throughout
learning processes such as classes, tests, and evaluations.

===== PDF_PAGE 240 =====
218 2021년 중국(중화권) 첨단기술 모니터링 및 DB 구축 사업: 스마트 에듀·디지털 헬스케어
2. Digital Healthcare
Digital transformation is in full motion and digital transformation in the healthcare sector
is accelerating further with the spread of COVID-19. Digital healthcare means the
combination of IT innovation technology and medical technology to provide improvement
or new forms of medical services or to create innovation in the medical system. Digital
healthcare includes various fields such as telemedicine, mobile health, health care analysis,
and digital health care systems.
1.1. Environmental Changes
(1) Technological changes: As advanced ICT technology began to be applied throughout
healthcare, better service provision became possible. Telemedicine using 5G technology,
surgical training using VR, and integrated data management have become possible. The
probability of disease was reduced by predicting through big data, and the efficiency of
operation of medical institutions was also increased. By applying AI technology, the scope
of use in overall medical care, such as medical robots and new drug development, has been
expanded.
(2) Demand changes: Among the healthcare industries in China, the size of the digital
healthcare market is expected to surge from 2.1% in 2015 to 24.0% in 2030. The reason for
this is that problems such as changes in the demographic structure in China, the
concentration of medical care, the shortage of medical personnel, and high medical costs are
still unresolved.
(3) Institutional changes: The Chinese government’s ‘Internet+’ policy is also applied to
the medical field, actively promoting the convergence of digital healthcare and AI. It
encourages the use of AI in medical fields such as diagnosis assistance and disease
prevention through AI-related policies, and emphasizes the localization, smartization, and
development of large-scale medical AI products through government policies related to
health and healthcare.
2.2. Innovation Ecosystem
(1) Public actor driven ecosystem: The Chinese government is actively promoting the
establishment of a "smart hospital" to smartize hospitals. Smart hospitals can be largely
divided into basic infrastructure, smart medical care, smart services, and smart

===== PDF_PAGE 241 =====
Summary 219
management, and various private companies participate in each area, providing SW/HW,
major facilities and services to hospitals. However, in the process of establishing a smart
hospital, problems such as lack of funding, manpower resources, a multi-point system (a
system that doctors can treat at more than one medical institution), and deterioration of
hospital personnel and finances due to telemedicine need to be solved in the future.
(2) Private actor driven ecosystem: A digital healthcare ecosystem was created with the
entry of large (private innovation) platform companies and the introduction of digital
innovation technology. Platform companies are mainly developing core technologies such
as big data, medical information, and medical AI, and the establishment of their own
healthcare ecosystem is expected to be completed in the mid to long term. Representatively,
Pingan Healthcare Service and AliHealth are participating in many hospitals, pharmacies,
and companies, respectively, providing telemedicine, 1:1 counseling, and drug purchases to
consumers, and continuously upgrading database through vast amounts of medical
information.
In summary, public innovation is actively promoted by the government, but there are
problems such as large-scale expenditure, investment recovery, and inefficiency of profits
in smart hospital construction. In the case of private innovation, there are concerns that the
government will strengthen regulations on digital healthcare platforms centered on large
companies in the future by establishing an independent healthcare ecosystem centered on
platform companies.
Through a multi-layered analysis of smart education and digital healthcare, China’s
innovation system has been realizing innovation in various areas based on open regulations
on technology, unified markets, and high growth potential of private companies. Various
innovation actors, including the Chinese government and companies, have created various
success stories by utilizing the advantages of China’s unique innovation ecosystem.
However, the recent increase in the Chinese government’s regulatory intensity has put a
brake on the growth of the digital service sector.
The most fundamental problem is that as social problems in various service areas grew
due to existing open regulations, state intervention was needed (e.g., private education). It is
also due to concerns that excessive regulatory opening could significantly reduce the quality
of services and pose a serious threat to people’s lives (e.g., a doctor’s multi-point collective
system). Chinese innovators are also actively preparing countermeasures to these

===== PDF_PAGE 242 =====
220 2021년 중국(중화권) 첨단기술 모니터링 및 DB 구축 사업: 스마트 에듀·디지털 헬스케어
regulations and seeking new ways. For example, in the case of smart education companies,
government regulations are expected to shift the central axis of the project to relatively loose
comparative subjects, adult education, and early childhood education. In addition, it is
expected that the overseas expansion of companies will further increase, accelerating their
entry into Southeast Asia and Latin America, which are favorable to Chinese technology
and services.
Regarding changes in China’s policy stance and corporate response to smart education
and digital healthcare, Korea also needs to come up with the following countermeasures.
First, regarding the establishment of a domestic foundation for smart education, first, the
government should continuously build infrastructure and invest in related fields based on
consistent smart education policies. Second, it is necessary to improve the level of
non-face-to-face solutions and software of smart education and improve the overall
educational environment in school. Third, it is necessary to foster related industries such as
data by improving the legal system. In terms of global expansion, first, it is necessary to
promote the development of domestic smart education services through entry into overseas
markets based on the excellent technology of domestic companies. Second, the market is
expected to increase in the future by subdividing it in the process of entering overseas
markets, but the current educational infrastructure should establish a business model
development strategy targeting emerging countries. Third, it is also necessary to establish a
system base for overseas expansion (e.g., reorganization of data-related laws, establishment
of a legal system reflecting edutech trends, etc.). Finally, the support of private companies
and the global cooperation network should be enlarged.
Regarding the establishment of a domestic foundation in the digital healthcare field, it is
necessary to approve actions such as improving the data sharing system and connecting
with data secured by new IT companies. In addition, it is necessary to expand pilot projects
for digital healthcare only in underprivileged areas of hospitals, discover licenses dedicated
to digital, and train data professionals. From a mid- to long-term perspective, it is necessary
to promote the demonstration and confidence of Korean digital healthcare services by
promoting challenging medical innovation research involving various innovation subjects
and promoting projects to build genetic, living, hospital treatment, and medical data of the
entire population. Finally, to enter the global digital healthcare market, it is inevitable to
overhaul the current related system. Specifically, to speed up the establishment and

===== PDF_PAGE 243 =====
Summary 221
commercialization of the slow-paced integrated medical big data platform, regulations such
as linking clinical data centered on the big data platform and collecting biometric data
should be eased. In addition, legal issues related to the operation of AI related to digital
healthcare services should be clearly established, and government guidelines for
regulations on responsibility and conflicts between stakeholders should be established.
